Appendix A
Proof of Identification Result (
3.1
)

A.1
Assumptions for Identification

Here we restate the identification assumptions.

Assumption A.1

(
3.1
in the main paper)
.

(i)

(SUTVA.) There is no interference across participants, and the observed data equals the potential outcome under the observed treatment. Specifically, for each
t
,
X_{t}(\overline{a}_{t-1},\overline{m}_{t-1})=X_{t}
if
\overline{A}_{t-1}=\overline{a}_{t-1}
and
\overline{M}_{t-1}=\overline{m}_{t-1}
, and
M_{t}(\overline{a}_{t},\overline{m}_{t-1})=M_{t}
if
\overline{A}_{t}=\overline{a}_{t}
and
\overline{M}_{t-1}=\overline{m}_{t-1}
. In addition,
Y(\overline{a}_{T},\overline{m}_{T})=Y
if
\overline{A}_{T}=\overline{a}_{T}
and
\overline{M}_{T}=\overline{m}_{T}
.

(ii)

(Positivity when eligible.) For each
t
, there exists a positive constant
c>0
, such that if
P(H_{t}=h_{t},I_{t}=1)>0
then
c<P(A_{t}=a\mid H_{t}=h_{t},I_{t}=1)<1-c
for
a\in\{0,1\}
. Furthermore, if
P(H_{t}=h_{t},A_{t}=a)>0
, then
P(M_{t}=m\mid H_{t}=h_{t},A_{t}=a)>0
for all
m
in the support of
M_{t}
.

(iii)

(Sequential ignorability of
A_{t}
.) For each
t
, the potential outcomes
\big\{M_{t}(\overline{a}_{t})
,
X_{t+1}(\overline{a}_{t})
,
M_{t+1}(\overline{a}_{t+1})
,
X_{t+2}(\overline{a}_{t+1})
,
\ldots,X_{T}(\overline{a}_{T-1})
,
M_{T}(\overline{a}_{T})
,
Y(\overline{a}_{T},M_{T}(\overline{a}_{T})):\overline{a}_{T}\in\{0,1\}^{\otimes T}\big\}
are conditionally independent of
A_{t}
given
H_{t}
.

(iv)

(Sequential ignorability of
M_{t}
.) For each
t
, for any
a,b\in\{0,1\}
, and for any
m_{t}
in the support of
M_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})\perp Y\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),m_{t},\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}

A.2
Lemmas for Identificaftion

We state and prove a few useful lemmas.

Lemma A.1
.

Given any
t\in[T]
and any
a\in\{0,1\}
, if
A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}(H_{t})
then
M_{t}(D_{t}^{a})=M_{t}
and
Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}=Y
.

Proof of
A.1
.

By
A.1 (i)
,
M_{t}(\overline{A}_{t})=M_{t}
and
Y\big\{\overline{A},\overline{M}(\overline{A})\big\}=Y
. When
A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}(H_{t})
, we have
D_{t}^{a}=\overline{A}
, and therefore the lemma statement holds.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Given any
t\in[T]
, for any
a\neq b\in\{0,1\}
and any fixed
m_{t}
in the support of
M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b}),\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t}(\overline{A}_{t-1}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t}).

Proof of
A.2
.

First, note that
A.1 (iii)
implies that for any
m_{t}
and
m_{t}^{\prime}
in the support of
M_{t}
and any
a,b\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle Y\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),m_{t},\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\}\perp A_{t}\mid H_{t},M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}^{\prime}.

(A.1)

Now, for any
a,b\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b}),\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t}(\overline{A}_{t-1}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),m_{t},\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t},M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\Big]

(A.2)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),m_{t},\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\Big]

(A.3)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),m_{t},\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\Big]

(A.4)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),m_{t},\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}(D_{t}^{a})=m_{t}\Big]

(A.5)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{a}),\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}(D_{t}^{a})=m_{t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t}).

(A.6)

Here,
Eq.

A.2
follows from
A.1 (i)
,
Eq.

A.3
follows from
Eq.

A.1
,
Eqs.

A.4
and
A.5
both follow from
A.1 (iv)
, and
Eq.

A.6
follows from
A.1 (i)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Fix any
t\in[T]
and any
a\neq b\in\{0,1\}
. Let
f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})
denote the conditional density of
M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}
with respect to a dominating measure; likewise for
f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})
. We have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t})~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})}\frac{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t}\bigg\}

(A.7)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\mid H_{t})}\frac{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\mid H_{t},M_{t})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t},M_{t})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t}\bigg\}.

(A.8)

Proof of
A.3
.

We have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t})~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t})~f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})~\mathrm{d}m_{t}

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t})~\frac{f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}~f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~\mathrm{d}m_{t}

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t}\bigg\}~f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~\mathrm{d}m_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})}\frac{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t}\bigg\},

(A.9)

where the last equality follows from the law of total probability. This proves
Eq.

A.7
.

Furthermore, using Bayes’ Theorem we have

\displaystyle f(m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})=\frac{f(m_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})}=\frac{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid M_{t}=m_{t},H_{t})~f(m_{t}\mid H_{t})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})},

(A.10)

where
f(m_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})
and
f(m_{t}\mid H_{t})
represent the corresponding conditional densities with respect to a dominating measure. Using
Eq.

A.10
and its parallel version for
d_{t}^{b}
, and we have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})}\frac{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{f(M_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\mid H_{t})}\frac{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\mid H_{t},M_{t})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t},M_{t})}~Y~\bigg|~H_{t}\bigg\}.

(A.11)

Plugging
Eq.

A.11
into
Eq.

A.9
yields
Eq.

A.8
. This completes the proof.
∎

A.3
Proof of the Identification Result

The following is
3.1
in the main paper.

Theorem A.4

(Identification,
3.1
in the main paper)
.

Under
A.1
,
\theta^{aa}(t)
for
a\in\{0,1\}
is identified as

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{aa}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\big)\Big\}

(A.12)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t})}Y\bigg\}.

(A.13)

The mediation functional
\theta_{t}^{ab}
for
a,b\in\{0,1\}
and
a\neq b
is identified as

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t})\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\big\}\Big]

(A.14)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\mid H_{t})}\frac{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\mid H_{t},M_{t})}{P(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a}\mid H_{t},M_{t})}Y\bigg\}.

(A.15)

Proof of
A.4
.

Eqs.

A.12
and
A.13
are a special case of Theorem 1 in
Qian 2025
.

Now we show
Eqs.

A.14
and
A.15
. We present the proof for the setting where the support of
M_{t}
is discrete. For settings where the support of
M_{t}
is continuous, the proof is similar with
\sum_{m_{t}}
replaced by the integral.

We have

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{ab}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b}),\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b}),\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t}(\overline{A}_{t-1})\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg(\sum_{m_{t}}P\big\{M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\mid H_{t}(\overline{A}_{t-1})\big\}

\displaystyle~~~~\times\mathbb{E}\Big[Y\big\{D_{t}^{a},\overline{M}_{t-1}(D_{t}^{a}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b}),\underline{M}_{t+1}(D_{t}^{a})\big\}~\Big|~H_{t}(\overline{A}_{t-1}),M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{m_{t}}P\big\{M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\mid H_{t}\big\}~\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t})\bigg]

(A.16)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{m_{t}}P\big\{M_{t}(D_{t}^{b})=m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\big\}~\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t})\bigg]

(A.17)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{m_{t}}P\big(M_{t}=m_{t}\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\big)~\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t})\bigg]

(A.18)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}=m_{t})\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\big\}\Big].

(A.19)

Here,
Eq.

A.16
follows from
A.1
and
A.2
,
Eq.

A.17
follows from
A.1 (iii)
,
Eq.

A.18
follows from
A.1
, and
Eq.

A.19
follows from the definition of conditional expectation.

Thus, we have shown
Eq.

A.14
.
Eq.

A.15
follows directly from
A.3
. This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Deriving the Efficient Influence Function (EIF) for
\theta_{t}^{aa}
and
\theta_{t}^{ab}

We prove
4.1
in this section. We derive the EIF for
\theta_{t}^{aa}
in
Section

B.1
and the EIF for
\theta_{t}^{aa}
in
Section

B.2
. In both derivations, we first construct an influence function (and verify that it is indeed an influence function), then show that the constructed influence function lies in the tangent space (and therefore its projection onto the tangent space is itself), and thus it is the efficient influence function.

B.1
EIF for
\theta_{t}^{aa}

Fix
t\in[T]
. For notation simplicity, within this subsection we denote
Y_{0}:=Y(\overline{A}_{t-1},0,\underline{A}_{t+1})
,
Y_{1}:=Y(\overline{A}_{t-1},1,\underline{A}_{t+1})
,
A:=A_{t}
,
I:=I_{t}
and
H:=H_{t}
. By the causal consistency assumption,
Y=Y_{0}
if
A_{t}=0
and
Y=Y_{1}
if
A_{t}=1
. We consider the semiparametric model,
\mathcal{M}
, that consists of all probability distributions of
(H,A,Y_{0},Y_{1})
that satisfy the constraints that
A
must be 0 if
I=0
(recall
I\in H
) and that
A\perp(Y_{0},Y_{1})\mid H
. Suppose
P^{\star}\in\mathcal{M}
is the truth that generates the data. The following derivation extends the proof of Theorem 1 in
Hahn 1998
to causal excursion effects, where we tackle the technical challenges brought by the eligibility indicator
I
.

B.1.1
Step 1: Derive the score function in a parametric submodel

Denote a parametric submodel by
\{P_{\vartheta}:\vartheta\in\Theta\}
such that there exists
\vartheta_{0}\in\Theta
with
P_{\vartheta_{0}}=P^{\star}
. Note that we use
\vartheta
to parameterize the submodel to be consistent with
Hahn 1998
, and
\vartheta
is distinct from the
\theta
notation in
\theta_{t}^{aa}
. The density of
(H,A,Y_{0},Y_{1})
(with respect to some
\sigma
-finite measure) in this submodel is given by

\displaystyle f(h;\vartheta)~\pi(h;\vartheta)^{a}~\{1-\pi(h;\vartheta)\}^{1-a}~f(y_{0},y_{1}|h;\vartheta),

where
f(h;\vartheta)
denotes the marginal density of
H
,
\pi(h;\vartheta)=P_{\vartheta}(A=1|h)
, and
f(y_{0},y_{1}\mid h;\vartheta)
denotes the conditional density of
(Y_{0},Y_{1})
given
H
, respectively. The density of
(H,A,Y)
is then equal to

\displaystyle L(\vartheta)=f(h;\vartheta)~\{f_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)~\pi(h;\vartheta)\}^{a}~\{f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)~(1-\pi(h;\vartheta))\}^{a},

where
f_{1}(\cdot|h):=\int f(y_{0},\cdot|h)dy_{0}
and
f_{0}(\cdot|h):=\int f(\cdot,y_{1}|h)dy_{1}
. Equivalently, we rewrite the density as (recall
i
is the dummy argument for
I\equiv I_{t}
, the eligibility indicator)

\displaystyle L(\vartheta)=\begin{cases}f(h;\vartheta)~\{f_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)~\pi(h;\vartheta)\}^{a}~\{f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)~(1-\pi(h;\vartheta))\}^{a}&\text{ if }i=1,\\
f(h;\vartheta)~f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)&\text{ if }i=0.\end{cases}

The log-likelihood
\ell(\vartheta)
equals

\displaystyle\ell(\vartheta)=\begin{cases}\log f(h;\vartheta)+a\{\log f_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)+\log\pi(h;\vartheta)\}\\
\qquad+(1-a)\{\log f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)+\log(1-\pi(h;\vartheta))\}&\text{ if }i=1,\\
\log f(h;\vartheta)+\log f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)&\text{ if }i=0.\end{cases}

(B.1)

Therefore, when
i=1
, it follows from
Eq.

B.1
that the score function at the truth
\theta=\vartheta_{0}
equals

\displaystyle S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\partial\ell(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\dot{f}(h;\vartheta_{0})}{f(h;\vartheta_{0})}+a\bigg\{\frac{\dot{f}_{1}(y|h;\vartheta_{0})}{f_{1}(y|h;\vartheta_{0})}+\frac{\dot{\pi}(h;\vartheta_{0})}{\pi(h;\vartheta_{0})}\bigg\}+(1-a)\bigg\{\frac{\dot{f}_{0}(y|h;\vartheta_{0})}{f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta_{0})}-\frac{\dot{\pi}(h;\vartheta_{0})}{1-\pi(h;\vartheta_{0})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=t(h)+a~s_{1}(y|h)+(1-a)~s_{0}(y|h)+\frac{a-\pi(h)}{\pi(h)\{1-\pi(h)\}}~\dot{\pi}(h),

(B.2)

where

\displaystyle t(h):=t(h;\vartheta_{0}),

\displaystyle t(h;\vartheta):=\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\log f(h;\vartheta),

\displaystyle s_{1}(y|h):=s_{1}(y|h;\vartheta_{0}),

\displaystyle s_{1}(y|h;\vartheta):=\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\log f_{1}(y|h;\vartheta),

\displaystyle s_{0}(y|h):=s_{0}(y|h;\vartheta_{0}),

\displaystyle s_{0}(y|h;\vartheta):=\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\log f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta),

\displaystyle\pi(h):=\pi(h;\vartheta_{0}),

\displaystyle\dot{\pi}(h;\vartheta):=\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\pi(h;\vartheta).

(B.3)

When
i=0
, it follows from
Eq.

B.1
that the score function at the truth
\theta=\vartheta_{0}
equals

\displaystyle S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0}):=\frac{\partial\ell(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=t(h)+s_{0}(y|h).

(B.4)

Eq.

B.2
and
Eq.

B.4
imply that

\displaystyle S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})

\displaystyle=t(h)+i\bigg\{a~s_{1}(y|h)+(1-a)~s_{0}(y|h)+\frac{a-\pi(h)}{\pi(h)\{1-\pi(h)\}}~\dot{\pi}(h;\vartheta_{0})\bigg\}

\displaystyle~~~~+(1-i)s_{0}(y|h).

(B.5)

Note that
t(h),s_{1}(y|h),s_{0}(y|h),\dot{\pi}(h;\vartheta_{0})
all depend on the choice of the specific parametric submodel, whereas
\pi(h)=P(A=1|H=h)
does not depend on the choice of the parametric submodel.

B.1.2
Step 2: Derive the tangent space

Let
\mathcal{H}
denote the Hilbert space of 1-dimensional mean zero finite-variance measurable funtions of
(H,A,Y)
, equipped with the covariance inner product). (1-dimensional because
\theta_{t}^{aa}
is a scalar.) The tangent space
\mathcal{T}
is a linear subspace of the
\mathcal{H}
defined as the mean-square closure of parametric submodel tangent spaces, the latter being the 1-dimensional subspaces spanned by linear combinations of
S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})
. Using the same logic as the proof of Theorem 1 in
Hahn 1998
, we obtain based on
Eq.

B.5
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}=\bigg\{\tilde{t}(H)+I\Big[A\tilde{s}_{1}(Y|H)+(1-A)\tilde{s}_{0}(Y|H)+\{A-\pi(H)\}\tilde{b}(H)\Big]+(1-I)\tilde{s}_{0}(Y|H)\bigg\},

(B.6)

where
\tilde{t}(\cdot)
,
\tilde{s}_{1}(\cdot|\cdot)
, and
\tilde{s}_{0}(\cdot|\cdot)
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{t}(H)\}=0,\quad\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{s}_{1}(Y(1)|H)\mid H\}=0,\quad\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{s}_{0}(Y(0)|H)\mid H\}=0,

(B.7)

\tilde{b}(\cdot)
is any square-integrable measurable function of
h
, and
\pi(h)=P(A=1\mid H=h)
. Here,
\mathbb{E}
and
P
are with respect to the true
P^{\star}
.

B.1.3
Step 3: Prove pathwise differentiability

By definition of pathwise differentiability, it suffices to show that there exists a function
\varphi_{t}^{aa}
such that for any parametric submodel
\{P_{\vartheta}:\vartheta\in\Theta\}
with
P_{\vartheta_{0}}=P^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{aa}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=\mathbb{E}\{\varphi_{t}^{aa}S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})\},

(B.8)

with
\mathbb{E}
taken under
P^{\star}
. (Notation remark:
\varphi_{t}^{aa}
is distinct from but closely related to
\phi_{t}^{aa}
in the main paper, as we will demonstrate shortly.)

We first derive
\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}
. Consider the same parametric submodel as in Step 1. By
3.1
we have

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}\{\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}(Y\mid H,A=I)\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}\{I~\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}(Y\mid H,A=1)+(1-I)~\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}(Y\mid H,A=0)\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}\{I~\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}(Y_{1}\mid H)+(1-I)~\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}(Y_{0}\mid H)\}

\displaystyle=\int yf_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)if(h;\vartheta)dydh+\int yf_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)(1-i)f(h;\vartheta)dydh.

(B.9)

So using the identity
\frac{\partial f}{\partial\vartheta}=\frac{\partial\log f}{\partial\vartheta}f
, we obtain from
Eq.

B.9
that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta)}{\partial\vartheta}

\displaystyle=\int ys_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)f_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)if(h;\vartheta)dydh+\int yf_{1}(y|h;\vartheta)it(h;\vartheta)f(h;\vartheta)dydh

\displaystyle~~~~+\int ys_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)(1-i)f(h;\vartheta)dydh+\int yf_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)(1-i)t(h;\vartheta)f(h;\vartheta)dydh.

(B.10)

Evaluating
Eq.

B.10
at
\vartheta=\vartheta_{0}
and we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[Y(1)s_{1}\{Y(1)|H\}I]+\mathbb{E}\{\eta_{1}(H)It(H)\}

\displaystyle~~~~+\mathbb{E}[Y(0)s_{0}\{Y(0)|H\}(1-I)]+\mathbb{E}\{\eta_{0}(H)(1-I)t(H)\},

(B.11)

where
s_{1}(y|h),s_{0}(y|h)
and
t(h)
are defined in
Eq.

B.3
,
\eta_{1}(h):=\mathbb{E}\{Y(1)|H=h\}
, and
\eta_{0}(h):=\mathbb{E}\{Y(0)|H=h\}
.

Define

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{11}:=\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)\}+\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)-\theta_{t}^{11}.

(B.12)

B.2
, which is formally stated and proven later, implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=\mathbb{E}\{\varphi_{t}^{11}S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})\}.

Therefore,
\theta_{t}^{11}
is pathwise differentiable.

We now derive
\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}
. Consider the same parametric submodel as in Step 1. By
3.1
we have

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta)=\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}\{\mathbb{E}_{\vartheta}(Y\mid H,A=0)\}=\int yf_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)f(h;\vartheta)dydh.

So using the identity
\frac{\partial f}{\partial\vartheta}=\frac{\partial\log f}{\partial\vartheta}f
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta)}{\partial\vartheta}

\displaystyle=\int ys_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)f_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)f(h;\vartheta)dydh+\int yf_{0}(y|h;\vartheta)t(h;\vartheta)f(h;\vartheta)dydh.

(B.13)

Evaluating
Eq.

B.13
at
\vartheta=\vartheta_{0}
and we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=\mathbb{E}[Y(0)s_{0}\{Y(0)|H\}]+\mathbb{E}\{\eta_{0}(H)t(H)\}.

(B.14)

Define

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{00}:=\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=0)}{P(A=0|H)}\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=0,H)\}+\mathbb{E}(Y|A=0,H)-\theta_{t}^{00}.

(B.15)

B.3
, which is formally stated and proven later, implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=\mathbb{E}\{\varphi_{t}^{00}S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})\}.

Therefore,
\theta_{t}^{00}
is pathwise differentiable.

B.1.4
Step 4: Verify that the pathwise derivative is the EIF

We first verify that
\varphi_{t}^{11}\in\mathcal{T}
. For
\varphi_{t}^{11}
defined in
Eq.

B.12
, when
I=1
we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{11}=\frac{A}{\pi(H)}\{Y-\eta_{1}(H)\}+\eta_{1}(H)-\theta_{t}^{11}.

When
I=0
, we have
\pi(H)=0
and
A=0
, and thus

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{11}=Y-\theta_{t}^{11}.

Therefore, using the identities
I\eta_{1}(H)=I~\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)
and
(1-I)\eta_{0}(H)=(1-I)~\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{11}

\displaystyle=I\frac{A}{\pi(H)}\{Y-\eta_{1}(H)\}+I~\eta_{1}(H)+(1-I)Y-\theta_{t}^{11}

\displaystyle=IA\frac{Y-\eta_{1}(H)}{\pi(H)}+(1-I)\{Y-\eta_{0}(H)\}+\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)-\theta_{t}^{11}.

Therefore,
\varphi_{t}^{11}\in\mathcal{T}
with
\tilde{s}_{1}(y|h)=\frac{y-\eta_{1}(h)}{\pi(h)}
,
\tilde{s}_{0}(y|h)=y-\eta_{0}(h)
,
\tilde{t}(h)=\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)-\theta_{t}^{11}
, and
\tilde{b}(h)=0
. (We can easily verify that (
B.7
) holds for these
\tilde{s}_{1}
,
\tilde{s}_{0}
, and
\tilde{t}
.
\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{t}(H)\}=0
is true because of
3.1
.) Thus, by
Bickel et al. 1993
,
\varphi_{t}^{11}
is the EIF for
\theta_{t}^{11}
.

We now verify that
\varphi_{t}^{00}\in\mathcal{T}
. For
\varphi_{t}^{00}
defined in
Eq.

B.14
, using the fact that
(1-I)(1-A)=1-I
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{00}

\displaystyle=I\frac{1-A}{1-\pi(H)}\{Y-\eta_{0}(H)\}+(1-I)\frac{1-A}{1-\pi(H)}\{Y-\eta_{0}(H)\}+\eta_{0}(H)-\theta_{t}^{00}

\displaystyle=I\frac{1-A}{1-\pi(H)}\{Y-\eta_{0}(H)\}+(1-I)\frac{1}{1-\pi(H)}\{Y-\eta_{0}(H)\}+\eta_{0}(H)-\theta_{t}^{00}.

Therefore,
\varphi_{t}^{00}\in\mathcal{T}
with
\tilde{s}_{1}(y|h)=0
,
\tilde{s}_{0}(y|h)=\frac{1}{1-\pi(h)}\{y-\eta_{0}(h)\}
,
\tilde{t}(h)=\eta_{0}(h)-\theta_{t}^{00}
, and
\tilde{b}(h)=0
. (We can easily verify that (
B.7
) holds for these
\tilde{s}_{1}
,
\tilde{s}_{0}
, and
\tilde{t}
.
\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{t}(H)\}=0
is true because of
3.1
.) Thus, by
Bickel et al. 1993
,
\varphi_{t}^{11}
is the EIF for
\theta_{t}^{11}
.

This completes the derivation of the EIFs for
\theta_{t}^{11}
and
\theta_{t}^{00}
.

B.1.5
Lemmas for deriving the EIF of
\theta_{t}^{aa}

First, we state and prove a few auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma B.1
.

(i)

For any function
g(H,A,Y)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}g(H,A,Y)\bigg\}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big\{g(H,A,Y)\mid H,A=I\big\}\Big].

(ii)

For any function
g(H,A)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\Big\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)\Big\}g(H,A)\bigg]=0.

(iii)

For any function
g(H)
, we have
\mathbb{E}\{s_{1}(Y|H)g(H)\mid H,A=1\}=0
and
\mathbb{E}\{s_{0}(Y|H)g(H)\mid H,A=0\}=0
.

Proof of
B.1
.

B.1 (i)
follows from iterated expectation given
H,A
:

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}g(H,A,Y)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}g(H,A,Y)~\bigg|~H,A=I\bigg\}P(A=I|H)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big\{g(H,A,Y)\mid H,A=I\big\}\Big].

B.1 (ii)
follows from iterated expectation given
H,A
:

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\Big\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)\Big\}g(H,A)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\Big\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)\Big\}g(H,A)~\bigg|~H,A=I\bigg]P(A=I|H)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)~\Big|~H,A=I\Big\}g(H,A)\bigg]

\displaystyle=0.

B.1 (iii)
follows from the following: For
a\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}\{s_{a}(Y|H)\mid H,A=a\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[s_{a}\{Y(a)|H\}\mid H,A=a]

(causal consistency)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[s_{a}\{Y(a)|H\}\mid H]

(sequential ignorability)

\displaystyle=0.

(property of the score function)

This completes the proof.
∎

Now we state and prove the lemmas used in
Section

B.1.3
.

Lemma B.2
.

With
\dfrac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}
derived in
Eq.

B.11
,
\varphi_{t}^{11}
defined in
Eq.

B.12
, and
S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})
derived in
Eq.

B.5
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=\mathbb{E}\{\varphi_{t}^{11}S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})\}.

Proof of
B.2
.

Let

A
1

\displaystyle:=\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)\},

A
2

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H),

A
3

\displaystyle:=\theta_{t}^{11},

A
a

\displaystyle:=t(H),

A
b

\displaystyle:=IAs_{1}(Y|H),

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)\}IAs_{1}(Y|H)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\Big[\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)\}IAs_{1}(Y|H)~\Big|~H,A=I\Big]\bigg)

(due to
B.1 (i)
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}\{YIAs_{1}(Y|H)\mid H,A=I\}]

\displaystyle~~~~-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)IAs_{1}(Y|H)\mid H,A=I\}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[I\mathbb{E}\{Ys_{1}(Y|H)\mid H,A=1\}]-0

(due to
B.1 (iii)
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[I\mathbb{E}\{Y(1)s_{1}\{Y(1)|H\}\mid H]

(consistency, ignorability)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[IY(1)s_{1}\{Y(1)|H\}].

(iterated expectation)

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A=I)}{P(A=I|H)}\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)\}(1-I)s_{0}(Y|H)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\Big[\{Y-\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)\}(1-I)s_{0}(Y|H)~\Big|~H,A=I\Big]\bigg)

(due to
B.1 (i)
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(1-I)\mathbb{E}\{Ys_{0}(Y|H)\mid H,A=0\}]

\displaystyle~~~~-\mathbb{E}[(1-I)\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)s_{0}(Y|H)\mid H,A=0\}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(1-I)\mathbb{E}\{Ys_{0}(Y|H)\mid H,A=0\}]-0

(due to
B.1 (iii)
)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(1-I)\mathbb{E}\{Y(0)s_{0}\{Y(0)|H\}\mid H]

(consistency, ignorability)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(1-I)Y(0)s_{0}\{Y(0)|H\}].

(iterated expectation)

, we have:

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)t(H)\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{I\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)t(H)\}+\mathbb{E}\{(1-I)\mathbb{E}(Y\mid A=I,H)t(H)\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{I\eta_{1}(H)t(H)\}+\mathbb{E}\{(1-I)\eta_{0}(H)t(H)\},

where the last step used the identities
I\eta_{1}(H)=I~\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)
and
(1-I)\eta_{0}(H)=(1-I)~\mathbb{E}(Y|A=I,H)
.

\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\big)
,
\mathbb{E}\big(\hbox to18.99pt{\vbox to18.99pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 9.49443pt\lower-9.49443pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}

,

,
\mathbb{E}\big(\hbox to18.99pt{\vbox to18.99pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 9.49443pt\lower-9.49443pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

, and
\mathbb{E}\big(\hbox to18.99pt{\vbox to18.99pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 9.49443pt\lower-9.49443pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
