\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to18.39pt{\vbox to18.39pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 9.19585pt\lower-9.19585pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 12.45 0 C 12.45 6.87 6.87 12.45 0 12.45 C -6.87 12.45 -12.45 6.87 -12.45 0 C -12.45 -6.87 -6.87 -12.45 0 -12.45 C 6.87 -12.45 12.45 -6.87 12.45 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-8.75002pt}{-2.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{73}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\big)
, they are all equal to 0, because
A
3
is a constant,
B.1 (iii)
, the fact that
\mathbb{E}\{A-\pi(H)\mid H\}=0
, and the fact that
\mathbb{E}\{t(H)\}=0
.

Putting together all the calculation results gives exactly
\dfrac{\partial\theta_{t}^{11}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}
given in
Eq.

B.11
, and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

With
\dfrac{\partial\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}
derived in
Eq.

B.14
,
\varphi_{t}^{00}
defined in
Eq.

B.14
, and
S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})
derived in
Eq.

B.5
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{t}^{00}(\vartheta_{0})}{\partial\vartheta}=\mathbb{E}\{\varphi_{t}^{00}S_{\vartheta}(\vartheta_{0})\}.

Proof of
B.3
.

The proof is similar to (and simpler than) the proof of
B.2
, and is thus omitted.
∎

B.2
EIF for
\theta_{t}^{ab}
(
a\neq b
)

The derivation of the EIF for
\theta_{t}^{ab}
follows in a similar way as
Section

B.1
. We provide a sketch of the proof below.

First, we show that the function
\varphi_{t}^{ab}
, defined as follows, is the EIF for
\theta_{t}^{ab}
:

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~f_{t}(M_{t}|A_{t}=d_{t}^{b},H_{t})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})~f_{t}(M_{t}|A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},H_{t})}\Big\{Y-\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\Big\}

\displaystyle~~~~+\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\Big\{\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})-\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})\Big\}+\nu_{t}(a,H_{t}),

(B.16)

where
f_{t}(M_{t}|A_{t},H_{t})
is the conditional density of
M_{t}
given
A_{t},H_{t}
. (Note that
\varphi_{t}^{ab}
is distinct from
\phi_{t}^{ab}
.) The fact that
\varphi_{t}^{ab}
defined in
Eq.

B.16
is the EIF can be shown in a similar fashion as the proof of Theorem 1 in
Tchetgen & Shpitser 2012
. The unique challenge beyond
Tchetgen & Shpitser 2012
is the handling of the eligibility indicator
I_{t}
, which can be handled in a similar way as
Section

B.1
.

Second, we show that
\phi_{t}^{ab}-\theta_{t}^{ab}
with
\phi_{t}^{ab}
defined in
Eq.

4.3
of the main paper is equivalent to
Eq.

B.16
. This is proven using Bayes theorem in a way similar to Lemma 3.2 in
Farbmacher et al. 2022
.

Appendix C
Proofs for Asymptotic Theory and Multiple Robustness

C.1
Theorem Statement

Let
\gamma:=(\alpha,\beta)
denote the parameter of interest. Let
\zeta_{t}:=(p_{t},q_{t},\eta_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})
denote the nuisance functions corresponding to decision point
t
, and
\zeta:=(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\ldots,\zeta_{T})
. Recall that the estimating function
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\psi(\gamma,\zeta):=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\omega(t)\left[\begin{matrix}\big\{\phi_{t}^{10}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})-\phi_{t}^{00}(p_{t},\eta_{t})-f(t)^{T}\alpha\big\}f(t)\\
\big\{\phi_{t}^{11}(p_{t},\eta_{t})-\phi_{t}^{10}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})-f(t)^{T}\beta\big\}f(t)\end{matrix}\right].

(C.1)

We restate
4.3
(asymptotic normality) separately for
\hat{\gamma}
(the estimator without cross-fitting in Algorithm
1
) and
\tilde{\gamma}
(the estimator with
K
-fold cross-fitting in Algorithm
2
). We state explicitly the regularity conditions required for each estimator.
4.2
(consistency) is a by-product that will be established in
Section

C.5
.

Theorem C.1

(
4.3
in main paper,
\hat{\gamma}
part)
.

Suppose
3.1
hold and consider
\gamma^{\star}=((\alpha^{\star})^{T},(\beta^{\star})^{T})^{T}
defined in (
4.4
). For each
t\in[T]
, suppose that the fitted nuisance function
\hat{\zeta}_{t}=(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\eta}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})
converges in
L_{2}
to limit
\zeta_{t}^{\prime}=(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\eta_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})
. Let
\zeta:=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{T})
; similarly define
\hat{\zeta}
and
\zeta^{\prime}
.
Suppose that for each
t\in[T]
, the nuisance function estimates satisfy the following rates:

\displaystyle\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\eta}_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(C.2)

\displaystyle\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\nu}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(C.3)

\displaystyle\|\hat{q}_{t}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(C.4)

Furthremore, suppose the following regularity conditions hold:
C.1
,
C.2
and
C.3
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,V)\text{ as }n\to\infty,

with

\displaystyle V:=\mathbb{E}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})^{T}\}\mathbb{E}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}^{-1,T}.

(C.5)

V
can be consistently estimated by
\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})^{T}\}\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}^{-1,T}
.

Theorem C.2

(
4.3
in main paper,
\tilde{\gamma}
part)
.

Suppose
3.1
hold and consider
\gamma^{\star}=((\alpha^{\star})^{T},(\beta^{\star})^{T})^{T}
defined in (
4.4
). For each
t\in[T]
, suppose that
\hat{\zeta}_{kt}=(\hat{p}_{kt},\hat{q}_{kt},\hat{\eta}_{kt},\hat{\mu}_{kt},\hat{\nu}_{kt})
, the fitted nuisance functions using
B_{k}^{c}
, converges in
L_{2}
to same limit
\zeta_{t}^{\prime}
. Let
\zeta:=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{T})
; similarly define
\hat{\zeta}_{k}
and
\zeta^{\prime}
.
Suppose that for each
t\in[T]
, the nuisance function estimates satisfy the following rates for all
k\in[K]
:

\displaystyle\|\hat{p}_{kt}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\eta}_{kt}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(C.6)

\displaystyle\|\hat{p}_{kt}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\nu}_{kt}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(C.7)

\displaystyle\|\hat{q}_{kt}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{kt}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|=o_{P}(n^{-1/2})

(C.8)

Furthremore, suppose the following regularity conditions hold:
C.1
,
C.4
and
C.5
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,V)\text{ as }n\to\infty,

with
V
defined in
Eq.

C.5
.
V
can be consistently estimated by

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}_{n,k}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta}_{k})\big\}\bigg]^{-1}\bigg[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}_{n,k}\big\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta}_{k})\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta}_{k})^{T}\big\}\bigg]\bigg[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}_{n,k}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta}_{k})\big\}\bigg]^{-1,T},

where
\mathbb{P}_{n,k}
denotes the empirical average over observations from the
k
-th partition
B_{k}
.

C.2
Regularity Conditions

We present the regularity conditions in establishing asymptotic normality. Here we use
\zeta^{\prime}
to denote the
L_{2}
limit of the nuisance parameters, and
\mathcal{Z}
to denote the space of all possible
\zeta
.

Assumption C.1

(Regularity conditions)
.

(i)

Suppose that for fixed
\zeta
,
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\}=0
has a unique solution in terms of
\gamma
.

(ii)

Suppose the parameter space
\Theta
of
\gamma
is compact.

(iii)

Suppose the support of
O_{i}
is bounded.

(iv)

Suppose
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}
is a continuous function in
\gamma
.

(v)

Suppose
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
is continuously differentiable in
\gamma
, and the class
\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta):\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}\}
is uniformly bounded and bounded by an integrable function.

(vi)

Suppose
\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta):=\frac{\partial\psi(\gamma,\zeta)}{\partial\gamma^{T}}
is bounded by an integrable function.

(vii)

Suppose
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\psi(\gamma,\zeta)^{T}
is bounded by an integrable function.

(viii)

Suppose
\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}
is invertible.

We note that the regularity conditions in
C.1
can all be verified straightforwardly using the form of
\psi
in
Eq.

C.1
. We keep them as assumptions instead of proven statements, so that the technical theory developed below can be potentially applicable to other estimation procedures.

The following are additional assumptions needed for establishing the asymptotics for the non-cross-fitted estimator
\hat{\gamma}
.

Assumption C.2

(Convergence of nuisance parameter estimator)
.

Suppose there exists
\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
such that the following hold.

(i)

\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta}|\mathbb{P}\psi(\gamma,\hat{\zeta})-\mathbb{P}\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})|=o_{P}(1)
;

(ii)

\|\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\|^{2}:=\int|\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})|^{2}dP=o_{P}(1)
;

(iii)

|\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}|=o_{P}(1)
;

(iv)

|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})^{T}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})^{T}\}|=o_{P}(1)
.

Assumption C.3

(Donsker condition)
.

Suppose
\Psi:=\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta):\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}\}
and
\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta):\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}\}
are
P
-Donsker classes.

The following are additional assumptions needed for establishing the asymptotics for the cross-fitted estimator
\check{\gamma}
.

Assumption C.4

(Convergence of nuisance parameter estimator (cross-fitting))
.

Suppose there exists
\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
such that the following hold.

(i)

For each
k\in[K]
,
\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta}|\mathbb{P}\psi(\gamma,\hat{\zeta}_{k})-\mathbb{P}\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})|=o_{P}(1)
;

(ii)

For each
k\in[K]
,
\|\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta}_{k})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\|^{2}=o_{P}(1)
.

(iii)

For each
k\in[K]
,
|\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta}_{k})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}|=o_{P}(1)
;

(iv)

For each
k\in[K]
,
|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta}_{k})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta}_{k})^{T}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})^{T}\}|=o_{P}(1)
.

Assumption C.5

(Additional regularity conditions (cross-fitting))
.

Suppose
\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta):=\frac{\partial\psi(\gamma,\zeta)}{\partial\gamma^{T}}
and
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\psi(\gamma,\zeta)^{T}
are uniformly bounded.

C.3
Lemmas on Multiple Robustness

We state and prove lemmas that elucidates the robustness properties of the estimating functions
\psi
defined in
Eq.

C.1
.

Lemma C.3

(
\phi_{t}^{aa}
is doubly robust)
.

Fix any
t\in[T]
and
a\in\{0,1\}
. Consider
\phi^{aa}_{t}
defined in
Eq.

4.2
, whose sample average is used to estimate
\theta_{t}^{aa}
in Algorithm
1
:

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t},\eta_{t}):=\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}Y-\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})-p_{t}(a|H_{t})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}\eta_{t}(a,H_{t}).

Suppose either
p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
or
\eta_{t}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
, then
\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t},\eta_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{aa}
.

Proof of
C.3
.

When
p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
, for any
\eta_{t}
, we use the identification of
\theta_{t}^{aa}
in
Eq.

3.2
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t}^{\star},\eta_{t})\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{p_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t})}Y\bigg\}-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})-p_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t})}{p_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t})}~\bigg|~H_{t}\bigg\}\eta_{t}(a,H_{t})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}^{aa}-0=\theta_{t}^{aa}.

When
\eta_{t}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
, for any
p_{t}
, we use the identification of
\theta_{t}^{aa}
in
Eq.

3.1
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t},\eta_{t}^{\star})\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}\{Y-\eta_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})\}\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\{\eta_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})\}

\displaystyle=0+\theta_{t}^{aa}=\theta_{t}^{aa}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.4

(
\phi_{t}^{aa}
is rate doubly robust)
.

Fix any
t\in[T]
and
a\in\{0,1\}
. Consider
\phi^{aa}_{t}
defined in
Eq.

4.2
. Suppose either
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
or
\eta_{t}^{\prime}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
, then

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\big\{\phi_{t}^{aa}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{\eta}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t}^{\prime},\eta_{t}^{\prime})\big\}\Big|\lesssim\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\eta}_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\|.

Proof of
C.4
.

Using the law of iterated expectation, for any
p_{t}
and
\eta_{t}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}Y\bigg\}=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}\eta_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})\bigg\}=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{p_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}\eta_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})\bigg\},

(C.9)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}\eta(a,H_{t})\bigg\}=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{p_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t})}{p_{t}(a|H_{t})}\eta_{t}(a,H_{t})\bigg\}.

(C.10)

In the following, to reduce clutter, within this proof we use the shorthand notation:
1_{a}:={\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})
,
p_{a}:=p_{t}(a|H_{t})
,
\eta_{a}:=\eta_{t}(a,H_{t})
. Using this notation, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t},\eta_{t})=\frac{1_{a}}{p_{a}}Y-\frac{1_{a}}{p_{a}}\eta_{a}+\eta_{a}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\big\{\phi_{t}^{aa}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{\eta}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{aa}(p_{t}^{\prime},\eta_{t}^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1_{a}}{\hat{p}_{a}}Y-\frac{1_{a}}{p_{a}^{\prime}}Y-\frac{1_{a}}{\hat{p}_{a}}\hat{\eta}_{a}+\frac{1_{a}}{p_{a}^{\prime}}\eta_{a}^{\prime}+\hat{\eta}_{a}-\eta_{a}^{\prime}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{p_{a}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{a}}\eta_{a}^{\star}-\frac{p_{a}^{\star}}{p_{a}^{\prime}}\eta_{a}^{\star}-\frac{p_{a}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{a}}\hat{\eta}_{a}+\frac{p_{a}^{\star}}{p_{a}^{\prime}}\eta_{a}^{\prime}+\hat{\eta}_{a}-\eta_{a}^{\prime}\bigg)

(C.11)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\prime}}\Big(p_{a}^{\prime}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\star}-\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\star}-p_{a}^{\prime}p_{a}^{\star}\hat{\eta}_{a}+\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\prime}+\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\prime}\hat{\eta}_{a}-\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\prime}\eta_{a}^{\prime}\Big)\bigg\},

(C.12)

where
Eq.

C.11
follows from
Eqs.

C.9
and
C.10
.

When
p_{a}^{\prime}=p_{a}^{\star}
, the sum inside the parentheses in
Eq.

C.12
becomes

\displaystyle(p_{a}^{\star})^{2}\eta_{a}^{\star}-\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\star}-(p_{a}^{\star})^{2}\hat{\eta}_{a}+\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\prime}+\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\hat{\eta}_{a}-\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\prime}=p_{a}^{\star}(p_{a}^{\star}-\hat{p}_{a})(\eta_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\eta}_{a}).

When
\eta_{a}^{\prime}=\eta_{a}^{\star}
, the sum inside the parentheses in
Eq.

C.12
becomes

\displaystyle p_{a}^{\prime}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\star}-\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\star}-p_{a}^{\prime}p_{a}^{\star}\hat{\eta}_{a}+\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\star}\eta_{a}^{\star}+\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\prime}\hat{\eta}_{a}-\hat{p}_{a}p_{a}^{\prime}\eta_{a}^{\star}=p_{a}^{\prime}(p_{a}^{\star}-\hat{p}_{a})(\eta_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\eta}_{a}).

In both cases,
Eq.

C.12
is
\lesssim\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\eta}_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\|
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.5

(Useful equalities for establishing multiple robustness of
\phi_{t}^{ab}
)
.

Fix any
t\in[T]
and
a\neq b\in\{0,1\}
. Let functions with superscript
\star
denote the true function, and functions without superscript
\star
denote any generic function (those can also denote the estimated versions). We have

(i)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{q_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t},M_{t})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

(ii)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}Y\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

(iii)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{q_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

(iv)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{q_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})\right\}

(v)

\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})\right\}

Using the shorthand notation in the proof of
C.6
and
C.7
, these equalities can be written as (i)
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}\Big)
; (ii)
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}Y\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
; (iii)
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
; (iv)
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
; (v)
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}\Big)
.

Proof of
C.5
.

All equalities follow immediately from the law of iterated expectations and the definition of the true nuisance functions in
Eq.

4.1
. We present the detailed proof below.

Proof of (i):
We have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})~\bigg|~H_{t},M_{t}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{q_{t}^{\star}(a|H_{t},M_{t})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}.

Proof of (ii):
We have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}Y\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}Y~\bigg|~H_{t},A_{t},M_{t}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}~\mathbb{P}\left\{Y\mid H_{t},A_{t},M_{t}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}~\mathbb{P}\left\{Y\mid H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{a},M_{t}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}.

Proof of (iii):
We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})~\bigg|~H_{t},M_{t}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{q_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}.

Proof of (iv):
we have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{q_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\right\}

[because of (iii)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})~\bigg|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\right\}p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})\right]

\displaystyle~~~~+\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})~\bigg|~H_{t},A_{t}\neq d_{t}^{b}\right\}\{1-p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}~\mathbb{P}\Big\{\mu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t},M_{t})~\Big|~H_{t},A_{t}=d_{t}^{b}\Big\}\right]+0

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})\right\}.

[definition of
\nu_{t}^{\star}(a,H_{t})
]

Proof of (v):
We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})~\bigg|~H_{t}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\dfrac{p_{t}^{\star}(b|H_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})\right\}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.6

(
\phi_{t}^{ab}
is multiply robust)
.

Fix any
t\in[T]
and
a\neq b\in\{0,1\}
. Consider
\phi^{ab}_{t}
defined in
Eq.

4.3
, whose sample average is used to estimate
\theta_{t}^{ab}
in Algorithm
1
:

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})

\displaystyle:=\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})~q_{t}(b|H_{t},M_{t})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})~q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})}\Big\{Y-\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})\Big\}

\displaystyle~~~~+\frac{{\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{b})}{p_{t}(b|H_{t})}\Big\{\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})-\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})\Big\}+\nu_{t}(a,H_{t}).

Suppose one of the four conditions hold: (i)
p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (ii)
p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
; or (iii)
\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (iv)
\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. Then
\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{ab}
.

Proof of
C.6
.

To reduce clutter, within this proof we use the shorthand notation:
1_{a}:={\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})
,
p_{a}:=p_{t}(a|H_{t})
,
q_{a}:=q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})
,
\mu_{a}:=\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})
,
\nu_{a}:=\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})
. Similarly for those with subscript
b
. Using this notation, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}(Y-\mu_{a})+\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}(\mu_{a}-\nu_{a})+\nu_{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}Y-\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}+\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}-\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}+\nu_{a}.

Case (i):

p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}=q_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\star}}Y\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a})

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}^{ab}-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a})

(C.13)

\displaystyle=\theta_{t}^{ab}-0-0=\theta_{t}^{ab}.

Here, the first four terms in
Eq.

C.13
follow from the cross-world weighting identification
Eq.

3.4
and
C.5
(i)(iii)(v), respectively.

Case (ii):

p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}}Y\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}).

(C.14)

The first term in
Eq.

C.14
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}}Y\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
because of
C.5
(ii), and thus it cancels with the second term in
Eq.

C.14
. The third term in
Eq.

C.14
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\star})=\theta_{t}^{ab}
because of
C.5
(iii), (iv), and the mediation g-formula
Eq.

3.3
. The fourth term in
Eq.

C.14
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}\Big)=\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a})
because of
C.5
(v), and thus it cancels with the last term in
Eq.

C.14
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})=\theta_{t}^{ab}
in Case (ii).

Case (iii):

\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}=q_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}q_{a}^{\star}}Y\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\star}).

(C.15)

The first term in
Eq.

C.15
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}q_{a}^{\star}}Y\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
because of
C.5
(ii) and (iv). The second term in
Eq.

C.15
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}\Big)
due to
C.5
(i). The third term in
Eq.

C.15
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}\Big)
because of
C.5
(iii), and thus it cancels with the second term. The fourth term in
Eq.

C.15
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
because of
C.5
(v), and thus it cancels with the first term. The last term in
Eq.

C.15
is
\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\star})=\theta_{t}^{ab}
because of the mediation g-formula
Eq.

3.3
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})=\theta_{t}^{ab}
in Case (iii).

Case (iv):

\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}Y\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\star}).

(C.16)

The first term in
Eq.

C.16
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}Y\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
because of
C.5
(ii), and thus it cancels with the second term in
Eq.

C.15
. The third term in
Eq.

C.16
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
because of
C.5
(iii) and (iv). The fourth term in
Eq.

C.16
is
\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
because of
C.5
(v), and thus it cancels with the third term. The last term in
Eq.

C.16
is
\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\star})=\theta_{t}^{ab}
because of the mediation g-formula
Eq.

3.3
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\phi_{t}^{ab})=\theta_{t}^{ab}
in Case (iv).

Therefore, we have verified that
\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{ab}
in each of the four cases. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.7

(
\phi_{t}^{ab}
is rate multiply robust)
.

Fix any
t\in[T]
and
a\neq b\in\{0,1\}
. Consider
\phi^{ab}_{t}
defined in
Eq.

4.3
. Suppose one of the four conditions hold: (i)
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (ii)
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
; or (iii)
\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (iv)
\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. Then

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\big\{\phi_{t}^{ab}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})\big\}\Big|\lesssim\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\nu}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|+\|\hat{q}_{t}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|.

Proof of
C.7
.

To reduce clutter, within this proof we use the same shorthand notation as in the proof of
C.6
:
1_{a}:={\mathds{1}}(A_{t}=d_{t}^{a})
,
p_{a}:=p_{t}(a|H_{t})
,
q_{a}:=q_{t}(a|H_{t},M_{t})
,
\mu_{a}:=\mu_{t}(a,H_{t},M_{t})
,
\nu_{a}:=\nu_{t}(a,H_{t})
. Similarly for those with subscript
b
. Using this notation, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}(Y-\mu_{a})+\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}(\mu_{a}-\nu_{a})+\nu_{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}Y-\frac{1_{a}q_{b}}{p_{b}q_{a}}\mu_{a}+\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\mu_{a}-\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}}\nu_{a}+\nu_{a}.

This implies that

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\big\{\phi_{t}^{ab}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}Y\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}\hat{\mu}_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\hat{\mu}_{a}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\hat{\nu}_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\hat{\nu}_{a})

\displaystyle~~~~-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}Y\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{a}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{1_{b}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\prime}\Big)-\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}\hat{\mu}_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\hat{\mu}_{a}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\hat{\nu}_{a}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\hat{\nu}_{a})

\displaystyle~~~~-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\prime}\Big)-\mathbb{P}(\nu_{a}^{\prime}),

(C.17)

where
Eq.

C.17
follows from
C.5
(i), (ii), (iii), and (v).

We now add and subtract three terms into
Eq.

C.17
:
\mathbb{P}\Big(-\dfrac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
,
\mathbb{P}\Big(\dfrac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}\Big)
,
\mathbb{P}(-\nu_{a}^{\star})
. This gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\phi_{t}^{ab}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{ab}(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})\big\}=\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\lambda_{3},

(C.18)

with

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}\mu_{a}^{\star}-\frac{q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}\hat{\mu}_{a}-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\hat{\mu}_{a}\bigg),

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\hat{\nu}_{a}-\nu_{a}^{\star}+\hat{\nu}_{a}\bigg),

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\bigg(-\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}+\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\prime}-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}+\nu_{a}^{\star}-\nu_{a}^{\prime}\bigg).

Note that only
\lambda_{3}
involves the quantities
p^{\prime},q^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}
.

First, we show that
|\lambda_{1}|\lesssim\|\hat{q}_{t}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|
. We have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}(\mu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\mu}_{a})-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}(\mu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\mu}_{a})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}(q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}-\hat{q}_{a}q_{b}^{\star})(\mu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\mu}_{a})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}(q_{a}^{\star}\hat{q}_{b}-q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}+q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}-\hat{q}_{a}q_{b}^{\star})(\mu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\mu}_{a})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{b}\hat{q}_{a}}\Big\{q_{a}^{\star}(\hat{q}_{b}-q_{b}^{\star})-q_{b}^{\star}(\hat{q}_{a}-q_{a}^{\star})\Big\}(\mu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\mu}_{a})\bigg].

\displaystyle\implies|\lambda_{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\hat{q}_{t}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|.

(C.19)

Second, we show that
|\lambda_{2}|\lesssim\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\nu}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|
. We have

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}(\nu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\nu}_{a})-(\nu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\nu}_{a})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}-1\bigg)(\nu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\nu}_{a})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{b}}(p_{b}^{\star}-\hat{p}_{b})(\nu_{a}^{\star}-\hat{\nu}_{a})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\implies|\lambda_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\nu}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|.

(C.20)

Lastly, we show that
\lambda_{3}=0
under any of the four conditions in the lemma statement.

Case (i):

p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\nu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\nu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

(C.21)

\displaystyle=0,

where
Eq.

C.21
follows from
C.5
(iv).

Case (ii):

p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\star}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\nu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\star}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

(C.22)

\displaystyle=0,

where
Eq.

C.22
follows from
C.5
(iv).

Case (iii):

\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\star}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

(C.23)

\displaystyle=0,

where
Eq.

C.23
follows from
C.5
(iv).

Case (iv):

\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. In this case,

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{a}^{\star}q_{b}^{\prime}}{p_{b}^{\prime}q_{a}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle+\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\mu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{p_{b}^{\prime}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{b}^{\star}}{\hat{p}_{b}}\nu_{a}^{\star}

\displaystyle\bigg)

(C.24)

\displaystyle=0,

where
Eq.

C.24
follows from
C.5
(iv).

Therefore, we have shown that
\lambda_{3}=0
under any of the four conditions in the lemma statement. This combined with
Eqs.

C.18
,
C.19
and
C.20
gives the desired lemma result. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.8

(
\psi
is multiply robust)
.

Consider
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
, the estimating function for
\gamma=(\alpha,\beta)
, defined in
Eq.

C.1
. For each
t\in[T]
, suppose that the nuisance function
\zeta_{t}=(p_{t},q_{t},\eta_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})
satisfies one of the four conditions: (i)
p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (ii)
p_{t}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
; or (iii)
\eta_{t}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
,
\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (iv)
\eta_{t}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
,
\nu_{t}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. Then
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta)\}=0
.

Proof of
C.8
.

The definition of
\gamma^{\star}=(\alpha^{\star},\beta^{\star})
in
Eq.

4.4
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\omega(t)\left[\begin{matrix}\big\{\theta_{t}^{10}-\theta_{t}^{00}-f(t)^{T}\alpha^{\star}\big\}f(t)\\
\big\{\theta_{t}^{11}-\theta_{t}^{10}-f(t)^{T}\beta^{\star}\big\}f(t)\end{matrix}\right]\right)=0.

(C.25)

Under any of the four conditions in the lemma statement, we have the following due to the double robustness of
\phi_{t}^{aa}
and the multiple robustness of
\phi_{t}^{ab}
(
C.3
and
C.6
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{10}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{10},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{00}(p_{t},\eta_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{00},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{01}(p_{t},q_{t},\mu_{t},\nu_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{01},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\phi_{t}^{11}(p_{t},\eta_{t})\}=\theta_{t}^{11}.

(C.26)

Therefore, using the definition of
\psi
in
Eq.

C.1
, under any of the four conditions in the lemma statement we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta)\}=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\omega(t)\left[\begin{matrix}\big\{\theta_{t}^{10}-\theta_{t}^{00}-f(t)^{T}\alpha^{\star}\big\}f(t)\\
\big\{\theta_{t}^{11}-\theta_{t}^{10}-f(t)^{T}\beta^{\star}\big\}f(t)\end{matrix}\right]\right)=0,

where the last equality follows from
Eq.

C.25
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.9

(
\psi
is rate multiply robust)
.

Consider
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
, the estimating function for
\gamma=(\alpha,\beta)
, defined in
Eq.

C.1
. For each
t\in[T]
, suppose that the fitted nuisance function
\hat{\zeta}_{t}=(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\eta}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})
converges in
L_{2}
to limit
\zeta_{t}^{\prime}=(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\eta_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})
. Suppose that for each
t\in[T]
, the limit
\zeta_{t}^{\prime}
satisfies one of the four conditions: (i)
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (ii)
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
; or (iii)
\eta_{t}^{\prime}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
,
\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (iv)
\eta_{t}^{\prime}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
,
\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. Then

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}\Big|\lesssim\max_{t\in[T]}\bigg\{\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\Big(\|\hat{\eta}_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\|+\|\hat{\nu}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|\Big)+\|\hat{q}_{t}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|\bigg\}.

Proof of
C.9
.

By the form of
\psi
in
Eq.

C.1
, We have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\omega(t)\left[\begin{matrix}\Big(\big\{\phi_{t}^{10}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{10}(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})\big\}-\big\{\phi_{t}^{00}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{\eta}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{00}(p_{t}^{\prime},\eta_{t}^{\prime})\big\}\Big)f(t)\\
\Big(\big\{\phi_{t}^{11}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{\eta}_{t})-\phi_{t}^{11}(p_{t}^{\prime},\eta_{t}^{\prime})\big\}-\big\{\phi_{t}^{10}(\hat{p}_{t},\hat{q}_{t},\hat{\mu}_{t},\hat{\nu}_{t})+\phi_{t}^{10}(p_{t}^{\prime},q_{t}^{\prime},\mu_{t}^{\prime},\nu_{t}^{\prime})\big\}\Big)f(t)\end{matrix}\right].

Therefore, the Lemma conclusion follows immediately from
C.4
and
C.7
. This completes the proof.
∎

C.4
Additional Technical Lemmas for Non-Cross-Fitted Estimator

We state additional technical lemmas used in establishing asymptotic normality and prove them. These lemmas are generalizations of
Cheng et al. 2023
to estimating functions that are not necessarily globally robust, and they can potentially be applied to other statistical problems and estimation procedures.

We use
|\cdot|
to denote the absolute value of a scalar of the Euclidean norm of a vector depending on the argument.

Lemma C.10

(Well-separated zero)
.

Suppose the following regularity conditions hold:
C.1 (i)
,
C.1 (ii)
and
C.1 (iv)
. Then the unique zero
\gamma^{\star}
of the function
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}
is well-separated. That is, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that
|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|>\epsilon
implies
|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}|>\delta
.

Proof of
C.10
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, consider
\delta:=\inf_{\gamma\in\Theta:|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|\geq\epsilon}|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}|
. Because the parameter space
\Theta
is compact (
C.1 (ii)
),
\{\gamma:|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|\geq\epsilon\}\cap\Theta
is also compact. This combined with the fact that
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}
is a continuous function in
\gamma
(
C.1 (iv)
) implies that the infimum is attained, i.e.,
\inf_{\gamma\in\Theta:|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|\geq\epsilon}|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}|=\min_{\gamma\in\Theta:|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|\geq\epsilon}|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}|
. Because
\gamma^{\star}
is the unique zero of
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}
(
C.1 (i)
),
\min_{\gamma\in\Theta:|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|\geq\epsilon}|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}|>0
. Therefore, we proved the lemma by constructing a particular
\delta:=\min_{\gamma\in\Theta:|\gamma-\gamma^{\star}|\geq\epsilon}|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}|>0
.
∎

Lemma C.11

(Consistency of
\hat{\gamma}
)
.

Suppose
\mathbb{P}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})=0
. Suppose the following regularity conditions hold:
C.1 (i)
,
C.1 (ii)
,
C.1 (iii)
,
C.1 (iv)
,
C.2 (i)
and
C.3
. Then
\hat{\gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\gamma^{\star}
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof of
C.11
.

Consider an arbitrary
\epsilon>0
. We need to prove
\lim_{n\to\infty}P(|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star}|>\epsilon)=0
. Because
C.1 (i)
and
C.1 (ii)
hold, by
C.10
there exists
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle P(|\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star}|>\epsilon)\leq P[|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\zeta^{\prime})\}|>\delta].

Therefore, it suffices to prove that
|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\zeta^{\prime})\}|
converges in probability to 0.

Because
\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}=0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\zeta^{\prime})\}|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\zeta^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\zeta^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}|+|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}|

(C.27)

Next we show that both terms in
Eq.

C.27
are
o_{P}(1)
.

For the first term in
Eq.

C.27
, by
C.2 (i)
we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\zeta^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}|\leq\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta}|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta^{\prime})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma,\hat{\zeta})\}|=o_{P}(1),

(C.28)

For the second term in
Eq.

C.27
, because
\Psi
is a
P
-Donsker class (
C.3
) and thus a
P
-Glivenko-Cantelli class,
\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}}|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\psi(\gamma,\zeta)|=o_{P}(1)
. Therefore,
|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}|=|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})|=o_{P}(1)
.

Thus, we showed that both terms in
Eq.

C.27
are
o_{P}(1)
. This completes the proof. ∎

Lemma C.12

(Convergence of the derivative.)
.

Suppose the following regularity conditions hold:
C.1 (vi)
,
C.2 (iii)
and
C.3
. If a sequence of random variables
\bar{\gamma}_{n}
satisfies
\bar{\gamma}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\gamma^{\star}
, then
\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\right\}
.

Proof of
C.12
.

We have

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\big\}+\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\big\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}}|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta)|

\displaystyle~~~~+\bigg[\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\big\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\big\}\bigg]+\bigg[\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\big\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\big\}\bigg].

(C.29)

We now control each of the three terms in
Eq.

C.29
.

For the first term in
Eq.

C.29
,
C.3
implies that
\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta):\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}\}
is a
P
-Glivenko-Cantelli class and thus

\displaystyle\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}}|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta)|=o_{P}(1).

(C.30)

For the second term in
Eq.

C.29
, it follows from the fact that
\bar{\gamma}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\gamma^{\star}
(lemma assumption), the dominatedness of
\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
(
C.1 (vi)
), and the dominated convergence theorem for convergence in probability (see, e.g.,
Chung 2001
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\big\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\big\}=o_{P}(1).

(C.31)

For the third term in
Eq.

C.29
,
C.2 (iii)
states that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\big\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\big\}=o_{P}(1).

(C.32)

Plugging
Eqs.

C.32
,
C.31
and
C.30
into
Eq.

C.29
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\big\}-\mathbb{P}\big\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\big\}=o_{P}(1).

The proof is thus completed.
∎

Lemma C.13

(Convergence of the “meat” term.)
.

Suppose the following regularity conditions hold:
C.1 (v)
,
C.1 (vii)
,
C.2 (iv)
and
C.3
. If a sequence of random variables
\bar{\gamma}_{n}
satisfies
\bar{\gamma}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\gamma^{\star}
, then
\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})\psi(\bar{\gamma}_{n},\hat{\zeta})^{T}\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})^{T}\}
.

Proof of
C.13
.

The proof is identical to the proof of
C.12
, except that we need to establish the following:

•

\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\psi(\gamma,\zeta)^{T}
is continuous: this is
C.1 (v)
;

•

\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\psi(\gamma,\zeta)^{T}
is bounded by an integrable function: this is
C.1 (vii)
;

•

\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta)\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})^{T}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta)\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})^{T}\}=o_{P}(1)
: this is
C.2 (iv)
;

•

\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\psi(\gamma,\zeta)^{T}
takes value in a
P
-Glivenko-Cantelli class.

We establish the last bullet point now. We know
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
is uniformly bounded (
C.1 (v)
),
\Psi
is a
P
-Donsker class (
C.3
), and the product operation
f\cdot g
is a Lipschitz transformation. These three statements imply that
\Psi\times\Psi
is a
P
-Donsker class (see, e.g.,
Van der Vaart 2000
). Thus,
\{\psi(\gamma,\zeta)\psi(\gamma,\zeta)^{T}:\gamma\in\Theta,\zeta\in\mathcal{Z}\}\subset\Psi\times\Psi
is a
P
-Donsker class and thus a
P
-Glivenko-Cantelli class. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.14

(Lemma 19.24 of
Van der Vaart 2000
)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
is a
P
-Donsker class of measurable functions and
\hat{f}_{n}
is a sequence of random functions that take their values in
\mathcal{F}
such that
\int\{\hat{f}_{n}(x)-f_{0}(x)\}^{2}dP(x)
converges in probability to 0 for some
f_{0}\in L_{2}(P)
. Then
\mathbb{G}_{n}(\hat{f}_{n}-f_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}0
and hence
\mathbb{G}\hat{f}_{n}\rightsquigarrow\mathbb{G}_{P}(f_{0})
.

C.5
Proof of
C.1

We are now ready to establish the asymptotic theory. We first prove
4.2
.

Proof of
4.2
.

The theorem follows immediately from
C.8
and
C.11
.
∎

We now prove
C.1
, which is
4.3
in the main paper for the non-cross-fitted estimator
\hat{\gamma}
.

Proof of
C.1
.

Because
\psi(\gamma,\zeta)
is continuously differentiable in
\gamma
(
C.1 (v)
), the Lagrange mean value theorem implies that

\displaystyle 0=\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\hat{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}+\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma^{T}}\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\bar{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}\right](\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star}),

(C.33)

where
\bar{\gamma}
is between
\hat{\gamma}
and
\gamma^{\star}
.
Because
C.11
implies that
\hat{\gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\gamma^{\star}
and thus
\bar{\gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\gamma^{\star}
,
C.12
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}.

(C.34)

Because
\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}
is invertible (
C.1 (viii)
),
Eq.

C.34
implies that
\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\bar{\gamma},\hat{\zeta})\}
is invertible with probability approaching 1. Therefore,
Eqs.

C.33
and
C.34
imply that with probability approaching 1 we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star})=-[\mathbb{P}\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}]^{-1}[\sqrt{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}].

(C.35)

We now show that the second term in
Eq.

C.35
is asymptotically normal. The assumed convergence rates for the nuisance function estimates [
Eqs.

C.2
,
C.3
and
C.4
] imply that for each
t\in[T]
, the limit
\zeta_{t}^{\prime}
satisfies one of the four conditions: (i)
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (ii)
p_{t}^{\prime}=p_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
; or (iii)
\eta_{t}^{\prime}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
,
\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
q_{t}^{\prime}=q_{t}^{\star}
; or (iv)
\eta_{t}^{\prime}=\eta_{t}^{\star}
,
\nu_{t}^{\prime}=\nu_{t}^{\star}
and
\mu_{t}^{\prime}=\mu_{t}^{\star}
. Therefore,
C.8
implies that
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}=0
, and thus we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}+\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}-\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}+\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}.

(C.36)

For the term
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}
in
Eq.

C.36
, because of
C.2 (ii)
, we can invoke
C.14
to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}0,

or equivalently

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}+o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(C.37)

For the term
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}
in
Eq.

C.36
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\Big|\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})-\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{t\in[T]}\bigg\{\|\hat{p}_{t}-p_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\Big(\|\hat{\eta}_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\star}\|+\|\hat{\nu}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{\star}\|\Big)+\|\hat{q}_{t}-q_{t}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\star}\|\bigg\}

(C.38)

\displaystyle=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

(C.39)

where
Eq.

C.38
follows from
C.9
and
Eq.

C.39
follows from the assumed convergence rates for the nuisance function estimates [
Eqs.

C.2
,
C.3
and
C.4
].

Plugging
Eqs.

C.37
and
C.39
into
Eq.

C.36
and we get

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\hat{\zeta})\}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N\Big(0,~\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})^{T}\}\Big),

(C.40)

where the last step follows from the Lindeberg-Feller Central Limit Theorem and the fact that
\mathbb{P}\{\psi(\gamma^{\star},\zeta^{\prime})\}=0
(
C.8
).

Therefore, plugging
Eq.

C.40
into
Eq.

C.35
, it follows from Slutsky’s Theorem that
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma^{\star})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,V)
with
V
defined in the theorem statement. Consistency of the variance estimator follows immediately from
C.12
and
C.13
and the continuous mapping theorem. This completes the proof.
∎

C.6
Proof of
C.2

The proof for the asymptotic normality of the cross-fitting estimator is through verifying the conditions for Theorems 3.1 and 3.2 in
Chernozhukov et al. 2018
. The technical details are similar to the proof of Theorem 4.2 in
Farbmacher et al. 2022
, and much of the technical results reuse the proof of
C.1
. Thus the proof is omitted.

Appendix D
Additional Details and Results for Simulation

D.1
Deriving the True Nuisance Functions for GM-1

Because
P(A_{t}=a,M_{t}=m\mid H_{t})=s_{am}/s
, by marginalizing over
m=\{0,1\}
we obtain
p_{t}^{\star}
:

\displaystyle p_{t}^{\star}(1|H_{t})

\displaystyle=P(A_{t}=1\mid H_{t})

\displaystyle=P(A_{t}=1,M_{t}=1\mid H_{t})+P(A_{t}=1,M_{t}=0\mid H_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{10}+s_{11}}{s_{10}+s_{11}+s_{00}+s_{01}}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})}+e^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}{1+e^{\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})}+e^{\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}+e^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}},

and thus
p_{t}^{\star}(0|H_{t})=1-p_{t}^{\star}(1|H_{t})
.

To derive
q_{t}^{\star}
, we first derive

\displaystyle P(A_{t}=1\mid M_{t}=0,X_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})}}{1+e^{\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})}}=\text{expit}\{\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})\},

\displaystyle P(A_{t}=1\mid M_{t}=1,X_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}{e^{\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}+e^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}=\text{expit}\{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})\}.

Thus,

\displaystyle q_{t}^{\star}(1|H_{t},M_{t})=P(A_{t}=1\mid H_{t},M_{t})=\text{expit}\{\kappa_{0}M_{t}+\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})\},

and thus
q_{t}^{\star}(0|H_{t},M_{t})=1-q_{t}^{\star}(1|H_{t},M_{t})
.

Before deriving
\eta_{t}^{\star},\mu_{t}^{\star},\nu_{t}^{\star}
, we derive a few intermediate results.
We have

\displaystyle P(M_{t}=1\mid A_{t}=0,X_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}{1+e^{\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}=\text{expit}\{\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})\},

\displaystyle P(M_{t}=1\mid A_{t}=1,X_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}{e^{\kappa_{1}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})}+e^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+h_{1}(t,X_{t})+h_{2}(t,X_{t})}}=\text{expit}\{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})\}.

Thus,

\displaystyle P(M_{t}=1\mid H_{t},A_{t})=\text{expit}\{\kappa_{0}A_{t}+\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(t,X_{t})\}.

Furthermore, for each
t\in[T]
, we compute the following through numerical integration (by interagrating over the the distribution of
X_{t}
which is Gaussian):
\mathbb{E}(A_{t}),\mathbb{E}(M_{t}),\mathbb{E}(A_{t}M_{t})
, and we define
\delta_{t}:=\xi_{t}\mathbb{E}(X_{t})+\rho_{t}\mathbb{E}(M_{t})+\lambda_{t}\mathbb{E}(A_{t})+\tau_{t}\mathbb{E}(A_{t}M_{t})
.

Next we derive
\eta_{t}^{\star},\mu_{t}^{\star},\nu_{t}^{\star}
. We have

\displaystyle Y=\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})+\epsilon.

Then for each
s\in[T]
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{s}^{\star}(A_{s},H_{s},M_{s})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{s},A_{s},M_{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})

\displaystyle~~~~+\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})\mid H_{s},A_{s},M_{s}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})

\displaystyle~~~~+\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}\{(\xi_{t}\mathbb{E}(X_{t})+\rho_{t}\mathbb{E}(M_{t})+\lambda_{t}\mathbb{E}(A_{t})+\tau_{t}\mathbb{E}(A_{t}M_{t})\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})+\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}\delta_{t},

and

\displaystyle\nu_{s}^{\star}(a)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{s},A_{s}=a,M_{s})\mid H_{s},A_{s}=1-a\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})

\displaystyle~~~~+\xi_{s}X_{s}+\rho_{s}M_{s}+\lambda_{s}a+\tau_{s}aM_{s}+\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}\delta_{t}~\bigg|~H_{s},A_{s}=1-a\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})

\displaystyle~~~~+\xi_{s}X_{s}+\lambda_{s}a+(\rho_{s}+\tau_{s}a)\mathbb{E}(M_{s}\mid H_{s},A_{s}=1-a)+\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}\delta_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})

\displaystyle~~~~+\xi_{s}X_{s}+\lambda_{s}a+(\rho_{s}+\tau_{s}a)~\text{expit}\{\kappa_{0}(1-a)+\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(s,X_{s})\}+\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}\delta_{t},

and similarly

\displaystyle\eta_{s}^{\star}(A_{s})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Y\mid H_{s},A_{s})=\mathbb{E}\{\mu_{s}^{\star}(A_{s},H_{s},M_{s})\mid H_{s},A_{s}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}(\xi_{t}X_{t}+\rho_{t}M_{t}+\lambda_{t}A_{t}+\tau_{t}A_{t}M_{t})

\displaystyle~~~~+\xi_{s}X_{s}+\lambda_{s}A_{s}+(\rho_{s}+\tau_{s}A_{s})~\text{expit}\{\kappa_{0}A_{s}+\kappa_{2}+h_{2}(s,X_{s})\}+\sum_{t=s+1}^{T}\delta_{t}.

’s and the value of each cell displayed.

Appendix E
SleepHealth: Data Preparation and Covariate Selection

E.1
Data Sources and Participant Eligibility

We combined three streams of self-reported, sensor, and questionnaire data for each study participant:

•

Daily surveys
: sleepiness ratings, sleep-quality assessments, and PM check-ins.

•

Baseline questionnaires
: four instruments (
About Me
,
My Health
,
Sleep Assessment
,
Sleep Habits
) administered at enrollment.

•

HealthKit step counts
: minute-level step intervals recorded by participants’ Apple devices.

Participants were deemed eligible if they completed the sleepiness, sleep-quality, and PM check-in surveys on at least six consecutive calendar days, and if that first 6-day common engagement streak began within 30 days of their first survey entry. We identified streaks by inner-joining the date-stamped survey records, grouping by participant, and computing run lengths of consecutive dates. The resulting list of participant IDs and the start date of their qualifying streak comprised our eligibility anchor.

E.2
Data Integration and Processing

E.2.1
Baseline Covariates

For each eligible participant, we loaded all four baseline questionnaires and selected one record per instrument, choosing the entry whose timestamp was closest to the participant’s 6-day streak start. This ensured consistent temporal alignment between baseline covariates and the first sustained engagement period.

E.2.2
Longitudinal Daily Summaries

We restricted both the sleepiness and sleep-quality checkers to each participant’s inclusive 6-day window. Within each day, multiple sleep-quality checkers were averaged to produce a single daily mean and a corresponding observation count. (For sleepiness, most often there are two observations per day. For sleep-quality checkers, most often there are one observations per day.) The two daily summaries were then full-joined by participant and date, yielding a complete longitudinal dataset over days 1–6.

E.2.3
Step-Count Aggregation

From the AppleHealthKit data, we retained only intervals overlapping the same 6-day window. Each record’s duration and date span were computed, and total daily steps were summed by participant and start-time date. We then generated a full grid of all six days per participant and imputed zero for any missing day.

E.2.4
Final Data Assembly

A master file was created by left-joining the daily longitudinal summaries and baseline covariates, and then left-joining the daily-steps table.

E.3
Covariate Screening and Selection

E.3.1
Initial Screening

We began with all numeric variables in the assembled dataset (excluding identifiers, the binary exposure sleep-quality checkers, and the mediator sleepiness) and applied the following sequential filters:

1.

Missingness filter
: retained only variables observed in
\geq 70\%
of person-days (this only matters for baseline covariates).

2.

Variance filter
:

•

Continuous or categorical variables required standard deviation
\geq 0.01
.

•

Binary variables required proportion of ones between 1% and 99%.

3.

Collinearity filter
: eliminated one of each pair with Pearson
|r|>0.8

(using
caret::findCorrelation
).

E.3.2
Dual LASSO

We performed two separate LASSO regressions (via
glmnet
) with one-standard-error lambda (
\lambda_{\mathrm{1se}}
):

1.

Outcome model
: Gaussian to predict continuous sleep-quality score.

2.

Exposure model
: Binomial to predict the binary exposure.

The union of nonzero-coefficient predictors from both models formed our initial multivariable covariate set.

E.3.3
Discretization and Manual Recoding

Continuous predictors were discretized by selecting cutoffs that maximized the absolute
t
-statistic for the final day outcome. A suite of categorical items (e.g., income, education, smoking status, work schedule, fatigue frequency, total steps) was recoded into binary or grouped levels (e.g., “
\leq 6
hours” vs. “
>6
hours”, “zero steps” vs. “1–4500 steps” vs. “
>4500
steps”). Below is the detailed list of covariates and discretization used.

Variable

Question

Discretization

How hard is it for you (and your family) to pay for very basics like food, rent, mortgage, or heating, etc.?

Unknown if missing or code 6; Not very hard or better if response
>
3; Somewhat hard or worse if
\leq
3.

Unknown if missing or code 4; Not at all if response
>

\leq

\leq

Unknown if missing or code 3; Female if code 1; Male if code 2.

>

\leq
